KDF straightforward.\\nfunc hashPassword(password string) [sha256.Size]byte { return sha256.Sum256([]byte(password)) }\\n\\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\\nset -euo pipefail\\ngo run .\\n# D&D REST API\\n\\n## Running and verifying\\n\\nThe service requires Go 1.26 and the system `sqlite3` executable used by the\\nexisting persistence adapter. Start it in the foreground from the repository\\nroot:\\n\\n```sh\\nPORT=8080 ./run.sh\\n```\\n\\nThe process binds only to `127.0.0.1:$PORT` (and defaults to port `8080` when\\n`PORT` is unset). With it running, a quick health check is:\\n\\n```sh\\ncurl -i http://127.0.0.1:8080/health\\n```\\n\\nBefore changing code, use `gofmt -w main.go` and `go test ./...`; the package\\ncurrently has no Go test files, so the latter is also a compile check. Do not\\nstart a long-running server as part of automated verification.\\n\\n## Implementation map\\n\\n`main.go` is the complete application package:\\n\\n- `main` initializes persisted state, selects the port, and starts `net/http`.\\n- `newRouter` is the public routing inventory. Each entry is a method-qualified\\n  `http.ServeMux` pattern and maps directly to its handler.\\n- The handlers are grouped by domain: core dice/check/character helpers;\\n  combat sessions; authentication; storage administration; compendium monsters\\n  and items; campaign state; PHB rules; and DM tools.\\n- The storage helpers (`initializeStorage`, schema functions, `runSQLite`, and\\n  `querySQLite`) own the file-backed persistence boundary.\\n- The small helpers at the bottom handle strict JSON decoding and JSON error/\\n  success responses.\\n\\n`run.sh` is the supported launcher and deliberately runs `go run .` in the\\nforeground. `go.mod` pins the module to Go 1.26 with no third-party Go\\ndependencies.\\n\\n## State and persistence\\n\\n`game.db` is created in the current working directory. The storage adapter\\ninvokes `/usr/bin/sqlite3` and stores users, combat sessions, compendium\\nrecords, campaigns, campaign characters, and campaign events. JSON blobs are\\nused for structured records; users and combat sessions are also loaded into\\nthe process-local maps during startup. The storage status and reset endpoints\\nexpose the existing schema lifecycle.\\n\\nThree mutex-protected state areas exist: user state, combat state, and storage\\nstate. Where reset needs all three, it locks user state, then combat state,\\nthen storage state. Code that touches multiple areas must preserve this order\\nto avoid deadlocks. Persistence helpers called while user or combat state is\\nheld acquire only the storage mutex.\\n\\n## Safe extension conventions\\n\\nPreserve the existing API contract exactly: method, route pattern, status code,\\nJSON shape, validation message, and persistence behavior are observable.\\nRegister any accepted new route in `newRouter`; keep method-qualified patterns\\ninstead of adding ad-hoc method checks. Decode request bodies through\\n`decodeJSON`, which enforces a 1 MiB limit, rejects unknown fields, and rejects\\ntrailing JSON values. Send responses through `respondJSON` or `badRequest` to\\nretain the JSON content type and error format.\\n\\nKeep validation deterministic, use the existing storage helpers for durable\\nrecords, and test both successful and invalid requests. For persistence work,\\nalso verify restart and reset behavior using an isolated working directory so\\nthe local `game.db` does not leak state between test runs.\\n{\\n  \\\"suite\\\":
